GENERAL DESCRIPTION

The AD5940 and AD5941 are high precision, low power analog                referred to as the low power TIA, measures low bandwidth signals.
front ends (AFEs) designed for portable applications that require         The second TIA, referred to as the high speed TIA, measures high
high precision, electrochemical-based measurement techniques,             bandwidth signals up to 200 kHz.
such as amperometric, voltammetric, or impedance measurements.
The AD5940/AD5941 is designed for skin impedance and body                 An ultra low leakage, programmable switch matrix connects the
impedance measurements, and works with the AD8233 AFE in                  sensor to the internal analog excitation and measurement blocks.
a complete bioelectric or biopotential measurement system. The            This matrix provides an interface for connecting external transimpe-

                                                                          trix can also be used to multiplex multiple electronic measurement

and one common measurement channel, which enables a wide
capability of measurements of the sensor under test. The first            A precision 1.82 V and 2.5 V on-chip reference source is available.

tentiostat. One output of the DAC controls the noninverting input of      peripherals.

ADC RMS NOISE SPECIFICATIONS
The internal 1.82 V reference is used for all measurements.
Note that there is a correction factor of 1.835 V introduced to the ADC code to voltage conversion as a result of the ADC calibration. Although
a 1.82 V reference is used to calibrate the ADC offset and gain, 1.835/1.82 factor is needed to be multiplied during the calibration. If the ADC is
calibrated and there is a hardware reset, the calibration registers clear to the default value. The low power TIA gain resistor must be calibrated
regularly because this resistor has a high temperature drift.

ADC RMS Noise: Digital Filter Settings
Table 2 provides the rms noise specifications for the ADC with different ADC digital filter settings.

ADC RMS Noise: Peak-to-Peak Effective Bits
Table 3 provides the rms and peak-to-peak effective bits based on the noise results in Table 2 for various PGA gain settings (peak-to-peak
effective bits results are shown in parentheses). To calculate the rms bits, use the following equation:
analog.com                                                                                                                                                    Rev. E | 16 of 142
Data Sheet                                                                                                                     AD5940/AD5941
SPECIFICATIONS

log2 ((2 × Input Range)/RMS Noise)                                                                                                                                     (1)
where:
Input Range is the input voltage range to the ADC.
RMS Noise is the rms of the noise.
To calculate the peak-to-peak effective bits, use the following equation:
log2 ((2 × Input Range)/(6.6 × RMS Noise))                                                                                                                             (2)

SPI TIMING SPECIFICATIONS
MOSI and MISO are launched on the falling edge of SCLK and sampled on the rising edge of SCLK by the host and the AD5940/AD5941,
respectively. IOVDD = 2.8 V − 3.6 V and 1.8 V ±10%, unless otherwise noted.

THEORY OF OPERATION

The main blocks of the AD5940/AD5941 are as follows:                                     ► Programmable switch matrix. The input switching of the AD5940/
                                                                                           AD5941 allows full configurability in the connections of the exter-
► Low power, dual-output, string DAC used to set the sensor bias
                                                                                           nal sensors (see the Programmable Switch Matrix section).
  voltage and low frequency excitation. Supports chronoampero-
                                                                                         ► Programmable sequencer (see the Sequencer section).
  metric and voltammetry electrochemical techniques.
                                                                                         ► SPI interface.
► Low power potentiostat that applies the bias voltage to the
  sensor.                                                                                ► Waveform generator designed to create sinusoid and trapezoid
► Low power TIA that performs low bandwidth current measure-                               waveforms up to 200 kHz (see the Waveform Generator section).
  ments.                                                                                 ► Interrupt sources that output to a GPIOx pin to alert the host
► High speed DAC and amplifier designed to generate excitation                             controller that an interrupt event occurred (see the Interrupts
  signals for impedance measurements up to 200 kHz.                                        section).
► High speed TIA that supports wider signal bandwidth measure-                           ► Digital inputs/outputs (see the Digital Inputs/Outputs section).
  ments.
► High performance ADC circuit (see the High Performance ADC
  Circuit section).

LOW POWER DAC

Electrochemical Impedance Spectroscopy                                                 Low Power DAC in 4-Wire Isolated Impedance
                                                                                       Measurements
In many electrochemical applications, there is significant value
in carrying out a diagnostic measurement. A typical diagnostic                         For 4-wire isolated impedance measurements, such as body impe-
technique is to carry out an impedance measurement on the                              dance measurements, a high frequency sinusoidal waveform is
sensor. For some sensor types, the dc bias on the sensor must                          applied to the sensor via the high speed DAC. A common-mode
be maintained during the impedance measurement. The AD5940/                            voltage is set across the sensor using the low power DAC 6-bit

The high speed DAC is not calibrated during production testing by            voltage.
Analog Devices. This section describes the steps to calibrate the
high speed DAC for all gain settings and in both high power and low
power modes.
Calibrate the high speed DAC when the DAC is needed to generate
an excitation signal to a sensor. If an offset error exists on the
excitation signal, and a current or voltage output requires meas-
urement, the excitation signal can exceed the headroom of the
selected TIA, ADC input buffer, or PGA setting.
Figure 27 shows the circuit diagram for high speed DAC calibration.
A precision external resistor, RCAL, is required between the RCAL0
pin and the RCAL1 pin. To calibrate the offset, the differential
voltage measured across the RCAL resistor must be 0 V.                                         Figure 26. High Speed DAC Transfer Function
Calibrate the high speed DAC with the required bit settings                  The AD5940/AD5941 software development kit includes sample
(HSDACCON, Bit 12 and Bit 0). For example, if the DAC is                     functions that demonstrate how to use the ADC to measure the
calibrated with HSDACCON, Bit 12 = 0 and HSDACCON, Bit 0                     differential voltage across the RCAL resistor and how to adjust the
= 0, and the user changes HSDACCON, Bit 12 to 1, an offset error             appropriate calibration register until the differential voltage is ~0
is introduced. Either the DACOFFSET register or DACOFFSETHS                  V. The AD5940/AD5941 software development kit is available for
register must be recalibrated for the new output range.                      download from the AD5940/AD5941 product page.
The gain calibration is optional and adjusts the peak-to-peak volt-
age swing. Alternatively, adjust the voltage swing by changing the
maximum and/or minimum DAC code.

HIGH SPEED TIA CIRCUITS

The high speed TIA measures wide bandwidth input signals up to
200 kHz.
The output of the high speed TIA is connected to the main ADC
mux, where this output can be programmed as the ADC input
channel.
This block is designed for impedance measurements in conjunction
with the high speed DAC and excitation amplifier.
HIGH SPEED TIA CONFIGURATION
The high speed TIA is disabled by default and is turned on by
setting AFECON [11] = 1. The high speed TIA has programmable
flexibility built into the input signal selection, gain resistor selection,
input load resistor selection, and common-mode voltage source.
                                                                                                       Figure 28. High Speed TIA Switches
Input Signal Selection
The input signal options are as follows:                                            External RTIA Selection

► The SE0 input pin.                                                                The high speed TIA has the option of selecting an external gain
                                                                                    resistor instead of the internal RTIA gain options. To perform this se-
► The AIN0, AIN1, AIN2, and AIN3/BUF_VREF1V8 input pins.
                                                                                    lection, connect one end of the resistor to the DE0 pin and connect
► The DE0 input pin, which has its own RLOAD/RTIA options and is                    the other end to AIN0, AIN1, AIN2, or AIN3/ BUF_VREF1V8. The
   user programmable.                                                               DE0 pin must be connected to the output of the high speed TIA.
Gain Resistor Selection                                                             To use the DE0 pin for the external RTIA value, set the following
                                                                                    register values:
The gain resistor (RTIA) options are 50 Ω to 160 kΩ for the DE0
input, and 200 Ω to 160 kΩ for all other input pins.                                ► DE0RESCON = 0x97.
                                                                                    ► HSRTIACON, Bits[3:0] = 0xF.

                                                                                    AIN0, AIN1, AIN2, or AIN3/BUF_VREF1V8 (whichever pin the
The load resistor (RLOAD) options are as follows:                                   resistor is connected to) must be connected to the inverting input of

► RLOAD02 and RLOAD04 are fixed 100 Ω for SE0 and AFE3.                             When DE0RESCON = 0x97, the RLOAD_DE0 and RTIA_DE0 resistors
► For the DE0 pin, RLOAD is programmable. The user can select                       are short circuit, which means that the external RTIA is connected

Common-Mode Voltage Selection
The high speed TIA common-mode voltage setting, on the positive
input to the high speed TIA amplifier, is configurable. The configura-
tion options are as follows:
► Internal 1.11 V reference source, which is the same as the
   VBIAS_CAP pin voltage.
► Low power DAC output (VZERO0).

HIGH PERFORMANCE ADC CIRCUIT

ADC CIRCUIT OVERVIEW                                                          ADC conversions are triggered by writing directly to the ADC con-
                                                                              trol register via the SPI interface, or by writing to the ADC control
The AD5940/AD5941 implements a 16-bit, 800 kSPS, multichannel                 register via the sequencer.
SAR ADC. The ADC operates from a 2.8 V to 3.6 V power supply.
The host microcontroller interfaces to the ADC via the sequencer or           ADC CIRCUIT DIAGRAM
directly through the SPI interface.
                                                                              Figure 30 shows the ADC core architecture. Figure 30 excludes
An ultralow leakage switch matrix is used for sensor connection               input buffering, gain stages, and output postprocessing.
and can also be used to multiplex multiple electronic measurement
devices to the same wearable electrodes.
The ADC uses a precision, low drift, factory calibrated 1.82 V
reference. An external reference source can also be connected to
the VREF_1V8 pin.

The ADC input stage provides an input buffer to support low input
current leakage specifications on all channels.
To support a range of current and voltage based input ranges, the
ADC front end provides a PGA and a TIA. The PGA supports gains
of 1, 1.5, 2, 4, and 9. The low power TIA supports programmable
gain resistors ranging from 200 Ω to 512 kΩ. The high speed TIA

analog.com                                                                                                              Rev. E | 55 of 142
Data Sheet                                                                                                     AD5940/AD5941
HIGH PERFORMANCE ADC CIRCUIT

                                                                               The output of the gain stage goes through an antialias filter. The
                                                                               cutoff frequency of the antialias filter is set by PMBW, Bits[3:2]. Set
                                                                               the cutoff frequency to suit the input signal bandwidth.
                                                                               The ADC output code is calibrated with an offset and gain correc-
                                                                               tion factor. This digital adjustment factor occurs automatically. The
                                                                               offset and gain correction register used depends on the ADC input
                                                                               channel selected.
                                                                               See the Low Power TIA section for details on how to configure the
                                                                               RLOAD, RTIA, and RFILTER resistor values. The low power TIA output
                                                                               has a low-pass filter consisting of RFILTER and an external capacitor
                                                                               connected to the AIN4/LPF0 pin. RFILTER is typically 1 MΩ and the
                                                                               external capacitor is recommended to be 1 µF, which provides a low
                                                                               cutoff frequency.

                                 PGA is 1

[14:0]      Value                       Gain calibration for PGA gain = 4. These bits provide ADC gain correction for the auxiliary input         0x4000    R/W
                                        channels. These bits are used for all channels except the TIA and temperature sensor channels when
                                        PGA gain = 4. This value is stored as a signed number. Bit 14 is the sign bit and Bits[13:0] represent
                                        the fractional part.
                               0x0000   0. Illegal value resulting in an ADC result of 0.
                               0x2000   0.5. ADC result multiplied by 0.5.
                               0x4000   1.0. ADC result multiplied by 1. No gain adjustment (default value).
                               0x4001   1.000061. Minimum positive gain adjustment.
                               0x7FFF   2. Maximum positive gain adjustment.
                               0x0001   0.000061. Maximum negative gain adjustment.
                               0x3FFF   0.999939. Minimum negative gain adjustment.

PRECISION VOLTAGE REFERENCES

This section describes the integrated voltage reference options                          There are both high power and low power buffers associated with
available on the AD5940/AD5941. The AD5940/AD5941 can gener-                             the 1.11 V and 1.82 V references. The high power buffers are
ate accurate voltage references for the ADC and DAC. There is a                          used when the ADC is in active mode and is converting. The low
1.82 V reference for the ADC and DAC and a 2.5 V reference for                           power buffers are used in hibernate mode to maintain the charge on
the potentiostat. The 2.5 V reference must be decoupled via the                          the decoupling capacitors to enable faster wakeup from hibernate
VREF_2V5 pin and the 1.82 V reference must be decoupled via                              mode.
the VREF_1V82 pin. There is a 1.11 V reference for the ADC input
bias. This reference must be decoupled via the VBIAS_CAP pin.                            Figure 37 shows the various voltage reference options available
                                                                                         and the register and bits that control these options.

SEQUENCER FEATURES                                                    The number of commands executed by the sequencer can be read
                                                                      from the SEQCNT register. Each time a command is read from
The features of the AD5940/AD5941 sequencer are as follows:           command memory and executed, the counter is increments by 1.
► Programmable for cycle accurate applications.                       Performing a write to the SEQCNT register resets the counter.
► Four separate command sequences.                                    The sequencer calculates the cyclic redundancy check (CRC) of
► Large 6 kB SRAM to store sequences.                                 all commands it executes. The algorithm used is the CRC-8, using
► FIFO for storing measurement results.                               the x8 + x2 + x + 1 polynomial. The CRC-8 algorithm performs
► Control via the wake-up timer, SPI command, or GPIO toggle.         on 32-bit input data (sequencer instructions). Each 32-bit input is
                                                                      processed in one clock cycle and the result is available immediately
► Various interrupts from user maskable sources.
                                                                      for reading by the host controller. The CRC value can be read

WAVEFORM GENERATOR

The AD5940/AD5941 implements a digital waveform generator for                        fOUT = fACLK × SINEFCW/230                                          (15)
generating sinusoid, trapezoid, and square waveforms. This section
describes how to use the waveform generator.                                         where:
                                                                                     fACLK is the frequency of ACLK, 16 MHz.
WAVEFORM GENERATOR FEATURES                                                          SINEFCW is Bits[23:0] in the WGFCW register.
The waveform generator features sine wave, trapezoid, and square                     The sinusoid generator includes a programmable phase offset
wave capabilities and can be used with the high speed DAC or the                     controlled by the WGOFFSET register. When enabled, the phase
low power DAC.                                                                       accumulator is initialized with the contents of the phase offset
                                                                                     register. After the sinusoid generator starts, the phase increment is
                                                                                     always positive.

                                                                                     THE LOW POWER DAC

[2:1]      TYPESEL                                 These bits select the type of waveform.                                                              0x0      R/W
                                             00    Direct write to the DAC. User code writes to the HSDACDAT register directly.
                                             10    Sinusoid. Sets the WAVEGENEN bit in the AFECON register to 1. The DAC outputs a sine wave.
                                             11    Trapezoid. Sets the WAVEGENEN bit in the AFECON register to 1. The DAC outputs a trapezoid
                                                   wave.
0          TRAPRSTEN                               Resets the trapezoid waveform generator. The output restarts from the beginning of the Delay 1       0x0      W
                                                   period, with an output corresponding to DC Level 1. The reset takes effect immediately. After the
                                                   trapezoid generator is reset, the bit value returns to 0.
                                              0    Disable reset of the trapezoid waveform generator.
                                              1    Enable reset of the trapezoid waveform generator.

SLEEP AND WAKE-UP TIMER

SLEEP AND WAKE-UP TIMER FEATURES                                     CONFIGURING A DEFINED SEQUENCE
                                                                     ORDER
The AD5940/AD5941 integrates a 20-bit sleep and wake-up timer.
The sleep and wake-up timer provides automated control of the        The sleep and wake-up timer provides a feature that allows a
sequencer and can run up to eight sequences sequentially in any      specific order of sequences to execute periodically. The order in
order from SEQ0 to SEQ3. The timer is clocked from the internal 32   which the sequences are executed is defined in the SEQORDER
kHz oscillator clock source.                                         register. There are eight available slots in this register, from A to H.
                                                                     Each slot can be configured with any one of the four sequences.
                                                                     Figure 50 shows an example of this feature. There are three
                                                                     defined sequences executed, SEQ1, SEQ2, and SEQ3, as shown
                                                                     in Figure 50.
                                                                     To configure the AD5940/AD5941 to implement this sequence or-
                                                                     der, implement the following register settings:
                                                                     1. SEQORDER, Bit SEQA = 1 (SEQ1)
                                                                     2. SEQORDER, Bit SEQB = 2 (SEQ2)
                                                                     3. SEQORDER, Bit SEQC = 3 (SEQ3)
                                                                     4. SEQORDER, Bit SEQD = 1 (SEQ1)
                                                                     5. CON, Bit ENDSEQ = 3 (end on sequence D)

           Figure 48. Sleep and Wake-Up Timer Block Diagram

SLEEP AND WAKE-UP TIMER OVERVIEW
                                                                                        Figure 50. Sequence Order Diagram
The sleep and wake-up timer block consists of a 20-bit timer
that counts down. The source clock is the 32 kHz, internal, low      RECOMMENDED SLEEP AND WAKE-UP TIMER
frequency oscillator.                                                OPERATION
                                                                     Analog Devices recommends the following procedure when using
                                                                     the sleep and wake-up timer to optimize performance and power
                                                                     consumption:
                                                                     1. Disable the timer sleep function by setting PWRMOD, Bit 2 to 0.
                                                                        The sleep wake-up timer does not put the device into hibernate
                                                                        mode. Instead, place the device in sleep mode by writing to the
                                                                        SEQTRG register at the end of the sequence. This sleep mode
             Figure 49. Sleep and Wake-Up Timing Diagram                optimizes power consumption.
                                                                     2. Enable the timer wakeup function by setting TMRCON, Bit 0 to
When the timer elapses, the device wakes up and runs a sequence         1.
automatically. Up to eight sequences can run sequentially.
                                                                     3. Enable the sequencer to trigger sleep by setting PWRMOD, Bit
When the timer elapses, the device returns to sleep. If the timer       3 to 1 and the SEQSLPLOCK register to 0xA47E5.
elapses before the sequence completes execution, the remaining       4. Set the final sequence in CON, Bits[3:1]. If only one sequence
commands in the sequence are ignored. Therefore, the user code          is used, select that sequence.
must ensure that the values in the SEQxSLEEPx registers are large    5. Write the sleep time and wake-up time to the SEQxSLEEPH,
enough to allow sequences to execute all commands.                      SEQxSLEEPL, SEQxWUPH, and SEQxWUPL registers.
It is recommended to use the wake-up timer to disable the timer      6. Configure the order in which sequences are triggered by using
sleep function (PWRMOD, Bit 2 = 0) and use the sequencer to             the SEQORDER register.
enter hibernate mode. Set PWRMOD, Bit 3 = 1 to enable the            7. Enable the timer by writing to CON, Bit 0 = 1.
sequencer to put the device in hibernate mode.
                                                                     When CON, Bit 0 = 1, the timer loads the values from the SEQx-
                                                                     WUPH and SEQxWUPL registers and begins counting down. When
                                                                     the timer reaches zero, the device wakes up and executes sequen-
                                                                     ces in the order specified in SEQORDER, Bits[1:0]. The timer loads
                                                                     the values from the SEQxSLEEPH and SEQxSLEEPL registers

INTERRUPTS

There are a number of interrupt options available on the AD5940/                    interrupt event by writing to the corresponding bit in the AFEGE-
AD5941. These interrupts can be configured to toggle a GPIOx pin                    NINTSTA register. It is only possible to write to this register via the
in response to an interrupt event.                                                  sequencer. Writing to the AFEGENINTSTA register when using the
                                                                                    SPI has no effect.
INTERRUPT CONTROLLER INTERUPTS
                                                                                    EXTERNAL INTERRUPT CONFIGURATION
The interrupt controller is divided into two blocks. Each block
consists of an INTCSELx register and an INTCFLAGx register. The                     Eight external interrupts are implemented on the AD5940/AD5941.
INTCPOL and INTCCLR registers are common to both blocks. After                      These external interrupts can be configured to detect any combina-
an interrupt is enabled in the INTCSELx register, the corresponding                 tion of the following types of events:

Analog Generation Interrupt Register—AFEGENINTSTA
Address 0x0000209C, Reset: 0x00000010, Name: AFEGENINTSTA
The AFEGENINTSTA register provides custom interrupt generation. Writing to this register is only possible using the sequencer. Writing to this
register using the SPI has no effect. Reading this register using the SPI does not return meaningful data.

The AD5940 features eight GPIO pins, GPIO0-GPIO7. The AD5941           remaining GPIOs are unaffected.
has three GPIO pins, GPIO0-GPIO2. The GPIOs are grouped in
one port, which is eight bits wide. Each GPIOx contains multiple       Bit Toggle
functions that are configurable by user code.
                                                                       The GP0 port has a corresponding bit toggle register, GP0TGL.
                                                                       Using the bit toggle register, it is possible to invert one or more
                                                                       GPIO data outputs without affecting other outputs within the port.
                                                                       Only the GPIOx pin that corresponds to the write data bit equal to 1
                                                                       is toggled. The remaining GPIOs are unaffected.

                                                                       Input/Output Data Output Enable
                                                                       The GP0 port has a data output enable register, GP0OEN, by
                                                                       which the data output path is enabled. When the data output enable
                                                                       register bits are set, the values in GP0OUT are reflected on the
                                                                       corresponding GPIOx pins.

                                                                       Interrupt Inputs
                                                                       Each GPIOx pin can be configured to react to external events.

POWER MODES

There are four main power modes for the AD5940/AD5941: active                        is turned off, the leakage from the ADC is reduced, which subse-
high power mode (>80 kHz), active normal mode (<80 kHz), hiber-                      quently reduces the current consumption in hibernate mode.
nate mode, and shutdown mode.
                                                                                     Optionally, the low power DAC, reference, and amplifiers can re-
ACTIVE HIGH POWER MODE (>80 KHZ)                                                     main active to maintain the bias of an external sensor. However,
                                                                                     current consumption increases.
Active high power mode (>80 kHz) is recommended when generat-
ing or measuring high bandwidth signals >80 kHz. The 32 MHz                          SHUTDOWN MODE
oscillator is selected to drive the high speed DAC and ADC circuits
to handle the high bandwidth signal. To enable high power mode,                      Shutdown mode is similar to hibernate, except the user is expected
use the following sequence:                                                          to power-down the low power analog blocks.

3. Switch the oscillator to 32 MHz.                                                  powered down simultaneously by writing to the LPMODECON

   sample rate.                                                                      bits corresponding to certain analog blocks. By setting these bits

                                       0     Disables the sequencer autosleep function.
                                       1     Enables the sequencer autosleep function.
2          TMRSLPEN                          Autosleep function by sleep and wake-up timer.                                                              0x0      R/W
                                       0     Disables the sleep and wake-up timer autosleep function.
                                       1     Enables the sleep and wake-up timer autosleep function.
[1:0]      PWRMOD                            Power mode control bits. When read, these bits contain the last power mode value entered by user            0x1      R/W
                                             code.
                                      01     Active mode. Normal working mode. All digital circuits powered up. The user can optionally power
                                             down blocks by disabling their input clock.
                                      10     Hibernate mode. Digital core powered down. Most AFE die blocks powered down (low power DACs
                                             and references can remain active to bias an external sensor). SRAM is powered down, with or without
                                             retention. The high speed clock is powered down. Only the low speed clock is powered up.
                                      11     Reserved.

Bits      Bit Name        Settings         Description                                                                                                   Reset    Access
[15:0]     PWRKEY                          PWRMOD key register. The PWRMOD register is key protected. Two writes to the key are necessary                0x0      R/W
                                           to change the value in the PWRMOD register: first 0x4859, then 0xF27B. Then, write to the PWRMOD
                                           register. A write to any other register before writing to PWRMOD returns the protection to the lock state.

CLOCKING ARCHITECTURE


Clock Divider Configuration Register—CLKCON0
Address 0x00000408, Reset: 0x0441, Name: CLKCON0

Table 172. Bit Descriptions for CLKCON0 Register
Bits       Bit Name         Settings      Description                                                                                              Reset    Access
[15:10]    Reserved                       Reserved. Do not write to these bits.                                                                    0x1      R/W
[9:6]      ADCCLKDIV                      ADC clock divider configuration. The ADC clock divider provides a divided clock from a 16 MHz root       0x1      R/W
                                          clock. The ADC clock frequency (fSYS) = root clock/ADCCLKDIV. The value can be 1 or 2.
[5:0]      SYSCLKDIV                      System clock divider configuration. The system clock divider provides a divided clock from a 16          0x1      R/W
                                          MHz root clock that drives most digital peripherals. The system clock frequency (fSYS) = root clock/
                                          SYSCLKDIV. The value range is from 1 to 32. Values larger than 32 are saturated to 32. Values of 0
                                          and 1 have the same results as divide by 1. The fSYS frequency must be ≤16 MHz.

Address 0x00000414, Reset: 0x0000, Name: CLKSEL

[15:4]     Reserved                           Reserved.                                                                                          0x0       R
[3:2]      ADCCLKSEL                          Selects the ADC clock source.                                                                      0x0       R/W
                                          0   Internal high frequency oscillator clock.
                                          1   External high frequency crystal clock.
                                         10   Internal low frequency oscillator clock (not recommended).
                                         11   External clock.
[1:0]      SYSCLKSEL                          Selects system clock source.                                                                       0x0       R/W
                                          0   Internal high frequency oscillator clock.
                                          1   External high frequency crystal clock.
                                         10   Internal low frequency oscillator clock (not recommended).
                                         11   External clock.

AUTOMOTIVE PRODUCTS
The AD5941W model is available with controlled manufacturing to support the quality and reliability requirements of automotive applications.
Note that this automotive model may have specifications that differ from the commercial models; therefore, designers should review the
Specifications section of this data sheet carefully. Only the automotive grade products shown are available for use in automotive applications.
Contact your local Analog Devices account representative for specific product ordering information and to obtain the specific Automotive
Reliability reports for this model.
